			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How Trane gets building insights 60x faster with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-trane-gets-building-insights-60x-faster-with-amazon-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Senthil Chinnaiyan]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 22 Sep 2026 15:30:34 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">dbd3f22e7d336a47137d152cb1d4539976157498</guid>

					<description>In about four weeks, Trane Technologies built an AI-powered agentic solution on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore that reduced a 20-minute, multi-screen building diagnostic workflow to a 20-second natural language interaction, a 60x improvement in time-to-insight. This post shares the architectural approach and key design decisions behind the solution.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Trane Technologies manages millions of connected heating, ventilation, and air conditioning (HVAC) assets worldwide, but getting a single operational answer could mean cross-referencing multiple dashboards and drilling through menus for 20 minutes or more. For organizations operating at this scale, that kind of friction slows operations, defers corrective action, and creates material business impact across the enterprise.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In 3–4 weeks, Trane’s engineering team built an AI-powered agentic solution on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; that reduced a 20-minute multi-screen diagnostic workflow to a 20-second natural language interaction. This is based on Trane’s internal benchmarking with technicians over several weeks. This represents a 60x improvement in time-to-insight, helping shift operations from reactive response to more proactive, data-driven optimization.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we describe the architectural approach and key design decisions behind the solution:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Separating agent logic from tool execution.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Integrating real-time telemetry through a centralized tool gateway.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Tailoring responses to different personas.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="trane-technologies-and-the-building-intelligence-challenge"&gt;Trane Technologies and the building intelligence challenge&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://www.tranetechnologies.com/en/index.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Trane Technologies&lt;/a&gt; is a global climate innovator with over $21 billion in annual revenue and operations in more than 100 countries. Through its strategic brand Trane, the company manages millions of connected HVAC assets, spanning data centers, hospitals, manufacturing facilities, and commercial real estate portfolios. At the heart of this vast landscape is &lt;a href="https://www.trane.com/commercial/north-america/us/en/products-systems/smart-building-technology/digital-platform.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Trane Cloud&lt;/a&gt;, a digital hub that aggregates real-time performance data from millions of HVAC systems. Trane Cloud transforms raw equipment telemetry into actionable intelligence for predictive maintenance, energy optimization, and operational excellence.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;While dashboard-based building management systems provide a foundation for monitoring and control, extracting cross-system insights can still require users to navigate multiple screens, layered menus, and disconnected dashboards. By combining natural language processing with deep integration into Trane Cloud, users can access that operational context through a single conversational interface. The result is faster answers to complex building management questions and a more proactive, informed approach to facility operations.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="business-challenge-why-building-operators-need-an-ai-agent"&gt;Business challenge: Why building operators need an AI agent&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Building operators, field technicians, and service managers have abundant data at their fingertips. Equipment telemetry, performance analytics, fault alerts, energy consumption patterns, and optimization opportunities flood in from disparate systems, yet extracting actionable insights remains difficult. The fundamental problem is that different stakeholders need radically different views of the same data.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Field technicians require diagnostic precision. They need refrigerant pressures, fault codes, and system-level troubleshooting workflows. Account managers need strategic intelligence. They need uptime metrics, cost savings opportunities, and portfolio performance trends. Building owners demand executive clarity. They need efficiency scores, sustainability metrics, and simplified operational summaries. Existing tools present a single interface across all roles, requiring each user to navigate features outside their workflow.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Existing building management applications rely on screen-by-screen navigation that makes cross-equipment comparison more challenging and demands that users memorize menu hierarchies and technical terminology. Even basic portfolio-level questions can require time-intensive manual workflows across multiple screens.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Together, Trane’s agentic AI solution and Trane Cloud deliver four capabilities:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Role-based access control&lt;/strong&gt; that tailors responses to each user’s permissions and needs.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Real-time HVAC analytics&lt;/strong&gt; providing instant access to current and historical performance data.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Intelligent search&lt;/strong&gt; across Trane’s knowledge base of technical documentation and best practices.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Extensible architecture&lt;/strong&gt; that evolves with advancing AI capabilities and expands to additional building systems.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;These capabilities create a more scalable way to access building intelligence across roles, workflows, and operational environments.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview-architecture"&gt;Solution overview: Architecture&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The challenge of scaling intelligent building operations lies in turning the massive volume of data generated by millions of connected assets into actionable insight. Although Trane Cloud ingests real-time telemetry at scale, answering operational questions has traditionally required users to navigate disconnected dashboards and manually connect information across systems. To solve this, the team built a conversational agent on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; and the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands framework&lt;/a&gt;, deployed through &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit (AWS CDK)&lt;/a&gt; infrastructure as code. The team chose Strands for the agent framework layer because it provides the developer SDK and orchestration logic for building agent behavior, while AgentCore handles the managed runtime, memory, tool gateway, and production infrastructure underneath.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To avoid the limitations of a monolithic design, the solution uses a multi-agent architecture where each specialized assistant is governed by its own system prompt, keeping it tightly focused on a single capability domain:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Resources Assistant&lt;/strong&gt; – Retrieves and summarizes reference material. For example, a user might ask: “Who do I contact, what manual should I follow, or what documentation can I share with the customer?”&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Knowledge Assistant&lt;/strong&gt; – Synthesizes technical answers about how equipment works, its system parameters, or whether Trane Cloud’s infrastructure is SOC 2 attested.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Analytics Insights Assistant&lt;/strong&gt; – Interrogates live telemetry to surface efficiency opportunities, flag items needing inspection, and trace fault root causes.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Expert Advisor&lt;/strong&gt; – Helps users decide which product solution fits a scenario, how to maximize customer value, or how to assemble a customized demo.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Navigation Assistant&lt;/strong&gt; – Returns the exact links and tools a user needs, from the tech support escalation form to the replacement-parts order page.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This architecture is designed for extensibility. Teams can connect additional agents or tools, such as work order management systems and enterprise customer relationship management (CRM) systems, through AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and open standards like the Model Context Protocol (MCP).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/16/ML-21136-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/16/ML-21136-1.png" alt="Trane’s multi-agent architecture on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, with specialized assistants connecting through AgentCore Gateway to Trane Cloud data" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: High-level architecture of Trane’s conversational agent on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h2 id="microservices-architecture-system-design-and-implementation-challenges"&gt;Microservices architecture: System design and implementation challenges&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Supporting these distinct user needs at enterprise scale requires an architecture that can evolve independently across capabilities. A monolithic agent would force every change (new tools, updated prompts, additional data sources) through a single deployment pipeline, creating bottlenecks as the system grows.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Reducing a 20-minute manual diagnosis to a 20-second conversation surfaced four architectural challenges. The first challenge was integration. The solution had to combine real-time telemetry with intelligent search across an extensive knowledge base while supporting connections to external systems like CRMs. The second was separation. Agent logic had to be untangled from backend tool execution so the two could deploy independently, with clear ownership boundaries. Third was context. The system needed to maintain conversational state across troubleshooting sessions without standing up complex custom vector database infrastructure. Fourth was observability. When an agent orchestrates multiple tools across a multi-step reasoning chain, failures become difficult to localize. A wrong answer could stem from a missing API credential, a malformed tool response, or a model hallucination. Without end-to-end tracing, the team had no way to distinguish between them at production scale.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="how-amazon-bedrock-agentcore-addresses-the-challenges"&gt;How Amazon Bedrock AgentCore addresses the challenges&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is an agentic platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. The Trane team used four AgentCore capabilities to address the preceding challenges.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before selecting AgentCore, the team evaluated hosting the agent on Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) and AWS Lambda. That approach would have required building session isolation, auto scaling logic, and per-session billing on top of the compute layer. Four differentiators drove the decision. First, the managed agent runtime alleviates infrastructure operations. There are no clusters to provision or scale, and no idle capacity to pay for between user sessions. Second, built-in session memory removes the need to stand up and maintain external vector databases or build custom context-window management code. Third, native tool orchestration through AgentCore Gateway turns existing internal APIs into agent-compatible tools without writing custom integration logic for each one. Fourth, AgentCore’s framework-agnostic design meant the team could use the Strands SDK without being locked into a proprietary orchestration layer, preserving flexibility as requirements change.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore runtime&lt;/strong&gt;: Trane uses AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, to isolate each user session in a dedicated microVM with its own CPU, memory, and filesystem. AgentCore runtime terminates and sanitizes each microVM on session completion. The team chose it because the microVM model separates the user-facing agent from the backend MCP Server, letting the two deploy independently with clear ownership boundaries. AgentCore runtime physically isolates a field technician’s session from a building owner’s, reinforcing role-based access without custom infrastructure. Trane pays only for active compute during a session. The long pauses between tool calls (typical of agentic workflows) don’t accumulate cost.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore Gateway&lt;/strong&gt;: Trane uses AgentCore Gateway to expose Trane Cloud’s internal APIs as MCP-compatible tools that the team organized by capability domain and integrated with Amazon OpenSearch Service. The team chose it because connecting the suite of assistants to real-time analytics, equipment telemetry, and issue diagnostics required a single access layer that handles authentication and schema translation. Future integrations (CRMs, work order systems) connect through the same Gateway without additional plumbing.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore memory&lt;/strong&gt;: Trane uses AgentCore memory, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, to maintain conversational state across troubleshooting sessions so users can ask natural follow-ups (“now compare that to last month”) without re-specifying context. The team chose it because the alternative was standing up a separate vector database and writing custom context-window management code. AgentCore memory provides short-term session memory out of the box, with a 90-day expiry lifecycle that balances contextual awareness with storage efficiency, helping Trane address their data retention policies.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore Observability&lt;/strong&gt;: Trane uses AgentCore Observability, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, to trace tool calls an agent makes and isolate failures across multi-step reasoning chains. The team chose it after encountering consistent tool failures during development that could not be identified without end-to-end visibility. Through the agent traces in Amazon CloudWatch, engineers confirmed the identical failure pattern across multiple invocations and traced it to a missing secret in AWS Secrets Manager. After adding the secret, the failures resolved.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The real-time data flow works as follows:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The user’s query, carrying a JSON Web Token (JWT), hits AgentCore runtime. The Runtime’s inbound authorizer (AgentCore Identity, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore) validates the token against the OpenID Connect (OIDC) discovery endpoint. AgentCore memory then injects previous conversation context before the agent processes the request.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The agent then triggers the separate MCP Server through OAuth 2.0 machine-to-machine authentication to securely access backend tools.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The MCP Server executes parallel tool calls to OpenSearch Service and other connected systems for rapid semantic data retrieval.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Finally, Anthropic Claude models on Amazon Bedrock synthesize the telemetry and stream the response back to the user through Server-Sent Events (SSE). This minimizes perceived latency, delivering answers in seconds. For model availability by AWS Region, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="results-and-impact"&gt;Results and impact&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Using Amazon Bedrock AgentCore and the Strands framework, the team achieved the 60x time-to-insight improvement described earlier. Cross-equipment comparisons and diagnostic workflows that once required navigating multiple screens now complete through a single natural language query in seconds. This helps teams move faster, reduce analysis steps, and take a more proactive approach to facility operations.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Beyond runtime performance, the architecture delivered additional benefits:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Rapid prototyping to production:&lt;/strong&gt; The team stood up a working prototype and demoed it to stakeholders within 3–4 weeks, then rolled the agent out first to internal field technicians as beta testers. Field technicians exercise the most demanding diagnostic workflows, so their feedback surfaced accuracy and usability gaps under real conditions. The team refined the agent responses based on that feedback before releasing to external customers.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Rapid integration across applications:&lt;/strong&gt; With decoupled microservices architecture and the centralized AgentCore Gateway, integrating the same agent backend into other enterprise applications took less than a day.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Effective production debugging:&lt;/strong&gt; The full invocation traces from AgentCore Observability in CloudWatch let the team pinpoint a recurring tool failure quickly.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="production-controls-and-responsible-ai"&gt;Production controls and responsible AI&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Deploying an AI agent that returns diagnostic recommendations from live operational data requires controls against misuse and out-of-scope responses. The team configured &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/guardrails/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; with content filters to block harmful or inappropriate outputs and topic denial policies that restrict the agent to its operational domain, helping restrict it from answering questions outside its operational domain. Sensitive information filters detect and redact personally identifiable information (PII) before responses reach the user, and prompt attack detection helps guard against jailbreak attempts that could bypass the agent’s system prompt.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At the infrastructure layer, Trane’s role-based access model helps restrict each user to data within their authorization scope. AgentCore runtime’s per-session microVM isolation helps prevent cross-tenant data leakage risk at the compute level. These controls work together so Trane can scale the agent to production users with confidence. Responses stay within scope, role-based access and PII filters help protect sensitive data, and Bedrock Guardrails help enforce the agent’s intended operational boundaries.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Trane’s implementation demonstrates how organizations can use AI agents and their proprietary data as a distinct competitive advantage. By building on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, the organization established a robust, secure enterprise standard that can be scaled and reused across different business units.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This phased rollout, from a 3–4 week prototype, through accuracy tuning and an internal beta, to external release and reuse, surfaced five insights for scaling enterprise AI:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Data is your differentiator&lt;/strong&gt;: The foundation of the agent’s success relies on existing enterprise data, such as OpenSearch Service indexes and Internet of Things (IoT) telemetry. The agent’s true value comes directly from using this proprietary data to drive more efficient energy usage and operational excellence.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Architectural foundation matters:&lt;/strong&gt; The Strands framework helped the team get started and iterate rapidly, while Amazon Bedrock AgentCore provided the purpose-built infrastructure and secure services necessary for running agents at scale. Committing to open standards like MCP helped provide flexibility for future enhancements and accelerated the build.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Phase your rollout, harden internally first:&lt;/strong&gt; Deploying to internal field technicians (the most demanding users) captured critical technical feedback and let the team refine accuracy before releasing externally to Trane Cloud customers.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Role-based access and responses:&lt;/strong&gt; Different roles require different tools and different response formats. Dynamic policy mapping means a field technician receives step-by-step diagnostic workflows while a building owner receives simplified efficiency scores. This drives adoption because users see responses tailored to their role.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Observability is key for agentic systems:&lt;/strong&gt; When an agent orchestrates multiple tools in a reasoning chain, failures can be hard to identify. End-to-end invocation tracing through CloudWatch gave the team the ability to pinpoint and resolve issues quickly.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To learn more about building agentic solutions, visit the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/what-is-bedrock-agentcore.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore documentation&lt;/a&gt;, explore the &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt;, or follow the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Artificial Intelligence Blog&lt;/a&gt; for more customer stories.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="whats-next"&gt;What’s next&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The team is expanding the solution in several areas.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;First, AgentCore Evaluations, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, will gate future rollout phases on automated accuracy thresholds. Easing the burden on the quality assurance (QA) team, the team will define pass-rate benchmarks that must clear before each release moves forward.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Second, Policy in Amazon Bedrock AgentCore will replace custom authorization logic currently coded into the Runtime. Tool-level access decisions will shift to declarative policy definitions, making it easier to onboard new roles and audit who can invoke which tools.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Third, the team is opening their AgentCore Gateway to other engineering teams across Trane. Because the Gateway exposes tools through MCP, an open standard, other teams can build their own agents on top of the same centralized data layer without learning proprietary interfaces or standing up duplicate integrations. The team is building self-service onboarding with usage tracking so they can measure adoption and identify which tools other teams find most valuable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Fourth, the team is adding new tools at a rapid pace. One example already in progress: field offices currently perform deep cost savings analyses manually for each customer site. The team is building this as an agent tool so the analysis runs on demand through natural language, removing hours of manual work per engagement.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How Tata Elxsi detects industrial safety risks in seconds on AWS</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-tata-elxsi-detects-industrial-safety-risks-in-seconds-on-aws/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Abhideep Rastogi]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 22 Sep 2026 15:19:54 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">bc39639b8f4e4cc7c356256fa699e460f9a01fa5</guid>

					<description>Learn how Tata Elxsi built IRIS, a real-time industrial safety platform on AWS. IRIS filters camera video at the edge, streams metadata through Amazon Kinesis, runs computer vision on Amazon SageMaker AI, and correlates detections into high-confidence alerts, detecting unsafe conditions in seconds instead of minutes.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Detecting industrial safety risks in seconds, not minutes, is what keeps workers safe on an active plant floor. This is what &lt;a href="https://www.tataelxsi.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Tata Elxsi&lt;/a&gt; set out to deliver by building IRIS, a real-time industrial safety platform on AWS.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we show how Tata Elxsi built IRIS (Industrial Real-Time Intelligence System). We cover the architecture decisions, the implementation approach, and the measurable results you can expect from a similar build. Whether you operate a handful of cameras or thousands across multiple sites, this blueprint provides patterns you can adapt for your organization.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="about-tata-elxsi"&gt;About Tata Elxsi&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://www.tataelxsi.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Tata Elxsi&lt;/a&gt; is a global provider of design and technology services across industries including automotive, manufacturing, broadcast, communications, healthcare, and transportation. Its teams combine engineering depth with AI and computer vision to help enterprises modernize safety-critical physical operations. IRIS is Tata Elxsi’s industrial vision platform, built for organizations that already operate camera infrastructure but cannot yet turn those feeds into real-time, actionable intelligence.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-customer-challenge"&gt;The customer challenge&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Industrial organizations have invested heavily in automated safety over the past decade. Manufacturing plants, warehouses, logistics hubs, and chemical facilities operate hundreds to thousands of cameras. These cover production lines, hazardous zones, vehicle corridors, loading areas, and restricted-access locations. Yet most of this footage is recorded and rarely acted on in real time. Safety teams face a common set of constraints:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Reactive monitoring&lt;/strong&gt; — Closed-circuit television (CCTV) functions as a recording system rather than a prevention system, so incidents surface only after they occur.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Human monitoring limits&lt;/strong&gt; — A control-room operator cannot reliably watch hundreds of feeds at once. Detection of unsafe conditions typically takes 15–45 minutes, depending on operator availability.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Inconsistent compliance&lt;/strong&gt; — Policy enforcement varies across shifts and sites, with audit coverage limited to two or three manual walkthroughs per shift.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Uneconomical scaling&lt;/strong&gt; — Adding cameras increases monitoring cost without a proportional improvement in safety outcomes.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;These aren’t failures of any single tool. They were signals that safety monitoring needs to evolve from passive recording to continuous, automated detection that scales with the number of cameras.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="why-real-time-computer-vision"&gt;Why real-time computer vision?&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Computer vision represents the next step in workplace safety. It doesn’t replace existing safety programs. It augments them with continuous, automated monitoring that runs around the clock. The design goal for IRIS was to analyze video as it’s produced, detect unsafe conditions automatically, and generate actionable alerts in near real time, without streaming raw video to the cloud. IRIS runs in the Asia Pacific (Mumbai) AWS Region, chosen for data-residency requirements and low-latency proximity to customer facilities in India.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tata Elxsi built IRIS as a serverless, event-driven pipeline that follows a repeatable pattern: observe at the edge, detect with computer vision, analyze for context, alert the right people, store for compliance, and learn from production data. Video is analyzed at the edge, only safety-relevant frames and structured metadata move to the cloud, and a correlation layer turns raw detections into high-confidence safety events. The following diagram shows how the components fit together.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/18/ML-19998-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/18/ML-19998-1.png" alt="Edge cameras and AWS IoT Greengrass feed Amazon Kinesis, then Amazon SageMaker AI inference, Lambda event correlation, alerting, and Amazon S3 storage" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: End-to-end IRIS architecture on AWS, from edge camera processing through streaming, inference, correlation, alerting, and storage&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="edge-acquisition-and-processing-filtering-at-the-source"&gt;Edge acquisition and processing: Filtering at the source&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The workflow begins at the edge. IRIS deploys a dedicated edge-compute tier using &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/greengrass/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS IoT Greengrass&lt;/a&gt; on industrial-grade, GPU-equipped edge servers, for example NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin or equivalent. Each server is installed at the facility and connected to the camera network over RTSP/ONVIF.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At the edge, IRIS extracts frames at a configurable rate of 2-5 frames per second and applies motion-based filtering. It then runs a lightweight first-pass model to identify frames that contain people, vehicles, or equipment. Frames that pass these filters are uploaded to &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3)&lt;/a&gt;. With AWS IoT Greengrass, you can manage secure device communication and deliver updated models to devices as Greengrass components from Amazon S3.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Decoupling the image path from the metadata path is central to the design. Extracted frames are written to a dedicated Amazon S3 bucket, partitioned by camera, date, and hour. The streaming event that flows through the pipeline carries only the Amazon S3 object key and context such as camera ID, plant, zone, and an NTP-synchronized timestamp. This keeps each event under 1 KB. A downstream consumer retrieves the referenced frame from Amazon S3 and runs the model. This keeps the streaming layer lightweight while the models retain full access to the visual data. In Tata Elxsi’s production deployments, filtering at the edge reduces the volume of frames sent to the cloud by roughly 70–80 percent, based on the customer’s production measurements.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="real-time-event-streaming-the-event-backbone"&gt;Real-time event streaming: The event backbone&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After edge processing, safety-relevant metadata and events are streamed into &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/kinesis/data-streams/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Kinesis Data Streams&lt;/a&gt;, which serves as the real-time event backbone of the platform. The stream carries frame metadata (the Amazon S3 object key), motion events, edge detection candidates, camera telemetry, and contextual safety information. It does not carry video.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because IRIS performs frame extraction at the edge, the cloud payload is structured event data with Amazon S3 references rather than continuous video. Amazon Kinesis Data Streams is purpose-built for this event-driven, metadata-first pattern, where sub-second latency on structured records is the priority.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The stream runs in on-demand capacity mode, which removes manual shard management and scales throughput automatically with event volume during shift changes or multi-incident bursts. In Tata Elxsi’s production deployments, sustained throughput is 2,000–5,000 events per second per deployment, with burst capacity to roughly 15,000 events per second. Measured event-ingestion latency is under 200 milliseconds at p95.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="vision-ai-inference-the-intelligence-engine"&gt;Vision AI inference: The intelligence engine&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Events are consumed by custom computer vision models deployed on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt;, the intelligence engine of IRIS. Separate real-time endpoints are provisioned per model family so each can scale independently:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Personal protective equipment (PPE) compliance&lt;/strong&gt; uses custom YOLOv8 object detection fine-tuned on industrial datasets to detect helmets, reflective jackets, gloves, and safety glasses.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Restricted-zone monitoring&lt;/strong&gt; combines object detection with polygon-based spatial geofencing for intrusion and boundary violations.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Worker safety analytics&lt;/strong&gt; uses SlowFast-based temporal action recognition for unsafe posture, movement, and interactions.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Vehicle and equipment proximity&lt;/strong&gt; uses multi-object tracking with monocular depth estimation for forklift and machine-proximity risks.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Endpoints run on ml.g5.xlarge instances (NVIDIA A10G GPU). AWS Application Auto Scaling applies a target-tracking scaling policy that scales out at 70 percent GPU utilization and scales in at 30 percent, with a minimum of two instances per endpoint for high availability (HA). To smooth traffic bursts across hundreds of concurrent streams, IRIS places an &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sqs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Simple Queue Service (Amazon SQS)&lt;/a&gt; queue between the stream consumers and the endpoints. Application Auto Scaling then adds instances when queue depth exceeds a configured threshold. Requests are processed in micro-batches of 4–8 frames to maximize GPU utilization. In production, each ml.g5.xlarge endpoint handles roughly 40-60 inference requests per second, and per-frame inference latency is under 300 milliseconds at p95.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="event-correlation-from-detections-to-high-confidence-events"&gt;Event correlation: From detections to high-confidence events&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A single detection is often not enough to act on. A worker briefly crossing a boundary might not warrant escalation, whereas repeated violations in a short window might require immediate intervention. IRIS therefore adds a correlation layer, implemented as &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt; functions that maintain short-term state in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon DynamoDB&lt;/a&gt; using time-to-live (TTL) entries for sliding-window evaluation. It combines detections with camera location, zone criticality, temporal patterns (configurable 30-second to 5-minute windows), and historical behavior, then evaluates violation frequency, duration, and severity. In Tata Elxsi’s production deployments, this correlation step reduces spurious alerts by an estimated 40–50 percent compared with passing detections through directly. This is based on the customer’s internal benchmarking of alert volumes before and after correlation.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="alert-generation-and-automated-response"&gt;Alert generation and automated response&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After a high-confidence event is identified, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt; functions run event-driven response workflows and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/step-functions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Step Functions&lt;/a&gt; manage multi-step escalation. Events are de-duplicated with a sliding window. The same detection type from the same camera within a configurable window (default 60 seconds) is consolidated into one alert. Events are then classified by severity based on zone criticality, confidence, and duration. Escalation follows defined service-level agreements:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Critical&lt;/strong&gt; — Alert within 5 seconds, escalate if unacknowledged within 2 minutes.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;High&lt;/strong&gt; — Alert within 10 seconds, escalate if unacknowledged within 5 minutes.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Medium&lt;/strong&gt; — Batched into digest notifications.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Low&lt;/strong&gt; — Logged for trend analysis, with no real-time alert.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon EventBridge&lt;/a&gt; Scheduler triggers escalation checks, and AWS Step Functions advance the state machine through supervisor, plant-manager, and safety-director levels as needed. Alerts are delivered through a real-time safety dashboard, email and SMS by severity and recipient group, webhook integration with enterprise IT service management systems, and mobile push notifications for supervisors and safety officers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="persistent-storage-and-compliance-the-system-of-record"&gt;Persistent storage and compliance: The system of record&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Every event, including detection results, alert records, metadata, and investigation evidence, is stored in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon S3&lt;/a&gt;, the system of record for the platform. Organizations use this repository for safety audits, compliance reporting, root-cause investigation, and regulatory review.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Lifecycle policies manage cost as data ages. Active event data stays in S3 Standard for 30 days. Historical events move to S3 Standard-Infrequent Access from 30 to 90 days. Compliance and investigation records transition to S3 Glacier Instant Retrieval from 90 days to 1 year, which allows millisecond retrieval for audits. Long-term archival moves to S3 Glacier Flexible Retrieval beyond 1 year, with expiration configurable per customer retention requirements. Extracted frames tied to confirmed events are retained for 1 year and then archived, and frames with no or below-threshold detections are purged after 7 days.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="continuous-learning-improving-with-production-data"&gt;Continuous learning: Improving with production data&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;IRIS improves as it runs. Production data in Amazon S3 feeds model-improvement workflows through &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt; training pipelines. Training data is roughly 80 percent real-world annotated data collected from production environments under customer data agreements. The remaining 20 percent is synthetic data generated for rare cases such as uncommon PPE, unusual lighting, and atypical camera angles. Annotation combines &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker-ai/groundtruth/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth&lt;/a&gt; for large-scale labeling with an in-house Tata Elxsi review team for edge cases. An active-learning loop routes low-confidence production predictions for human review.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Re-training runs on three separate triggers:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scheduled&lt;/strong&gt; — A quarterly baseline retraining cycle using accumulated production data.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Drift-based&lt;/strong&gt; — Model monitoring in Amazon SageMaker AI detects accuracy degradation and triggers re-training when accuracy drops below a configured threshold.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Feedback-driven&lt;/strong&gt; — Newly annotated samples above a threshold volume trigger an incremental training job.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Re-trained models are evaluated against a held-out evaluation set using the Amazon SageMaker AI model registry. Only models that meet or exceed current production accuracy are promoted, through blue/green deployment. As measured by Tata Elxsi on held-out production validation sets refreshed quarterly, PPE detection reaches 94.2 percent precision and 91.8 percent recall (mAP@0.5 of 92.7 percent). Restricted-zone intrusion reaches 96.1 percent precision and 93.4 percent recall. The post-correlation false-positive rate is under 3 percent across detection categories.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="security-privacy-and-compliance"&gt;Security, privacy, and compliance&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Security is enforced across a multi-account structure that separates model training, production inference, and analytics. Amazon S3 buckets use server-side encryption with customer-managed keys in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/kms/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS)&lt;/a&gt;, and inter-service communication uses TLS 1.2 or higher. Fine-grained &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/iam/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)&lt;/a&gt; policies scope each service role to least privilege, and human access uses &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/iam/identity-center/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS IAM Identity Center&lt;/a&gt; with roles aligned to job function.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Inference and data-processing workloads run inside a dedicated &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/vpc/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (Amazon VPC)&lt;/a&gt; with private subnets and no public internet exposure. VPC endpoints keep Amazon S3, Amazon Kinesis Data Streams, and Amazon SageMaker AI traffic on the AWS network. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudtrail/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CloudTrail&lt;/a&gt; records API activity, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/guardduty/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon GuardDuty&lt;/a&gt; monitors for anomalous access.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-results-measurable-business-impact"&gt;The results: Measurable business impact&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Across production deployments, IRIS moved customers from reactive surveillance to proactive safety management. Tata Elxsi reports the following outcomes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt;
 &lt;tbody&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Dimension&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Before IRIS&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;With IRIS (reported by Tata Elxsi)&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Unsafe-condition detection&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Manual review, 15–45 minutes&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Under 5 seconds, end to end&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Safety audit coverage&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;2–3 manual walkthroughs per shift&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Continuous, automated 24×7 coverage&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Recordable safety incidents&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Baseline&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;15–20% reduction in the first 6 months&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Manual surveillance operating cost&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Baseline&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Approximately 30% reduction&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Scaling model&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Cost grows with each added camera&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Hundreds of concurrent streams per site&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
 &lt;/tbody&gt;
&lt;/table&gt;
&lt;h2 id="key-takeaways-lessons-for-real-time-safety-platforms"&gt;Key takeaways: Lessons for real-time safety platforms&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Filter at the edge, stream metadata, not video&lt;/strong&gt; — Extracting frames at the edge and streaming only Amazon S3 references keeps the cloud pipeline lightweight and cuts data-movement cost, while the models still get full access to the image.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Correlation is what makes alerts trustworthy&lt;/strong&gt; — Raw detections produce noise. A temporal correlation layer turns them into high-confidence events and prevents the alert fatigue that causes teams to stop trusting the system.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Design for the model that will change&lt;/strong&gt; — A retraining loop driven by production data, drift monitoring, and active learning is what keeps accuracy high as sites, lighting, and camera angles vary.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Governance and privacy are day-one decisions&lt;/strong&gt; — For footage of identifiable people, anonymization, retention, and access control belong in the first design review, not the last.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;IRIS shows how existing camera infrastructure can become a real-time safety system on AWS, detecting unsafe conditions in seconds rather than minutes. By filtering at the edge, streaming metadata, running purpose-built models on Amazon SageMaker AI, and adding a correlation layer, Tata Elxsi built a platform that scales across hundreds of concurrent streams per site while keeping raw video out of the cloud. The same event-driven foundation extends to quality inspection, perimeter monitoring, and process observation, with new domain models and zone rules layered on without re-architecting the pipeline.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To explore building a similar solution, review the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/greengrass/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS IoT Greengrass&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/a&gt; documentation. To discuss a proof of concept for your facilities, contact Tata Elxsi or your AWS account team.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Extending public sector intelligence with Agentforce and AWS</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/extending-public-sector-intelligence-with-agentforce-and-aws/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Christian Ramirez]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 22 Sep 2026 15:17:45 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock Data Automation]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">5066ec9c015f24d80bf639cd7289221c5f05bc08</guid>

					<description>Public sector agencies process large volumes of unstructured evidence, such as body camera footage and scanned documents. This post shows how to combine Amazon Bedrock Data Automation with the Model Context Protocol (MCP) to turn that data into structured insights and surface them through natural language queries in Salesforce Agentforce.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Public sector agencies process large volumes of unstructured evidence, such as body camera footage, surveillance video, and scanned documents, that require extracting insights before anyone can act on them. This post shows how to combine &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/bda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Data Automation&lt;/a&gt; with the Model Context Protocol (MCP) to turn unstructured data into structured insights. You can then expose those insights through natural language queries in an AI agent, such as &lt;a href="https://www.salesforce.com/agentforce/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Salesforce Agentforce&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In our previous post, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/publicsector/modernizing-evidence-management-in-salesforce-public-sector-solutions-with-amazon-s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Modernizing evidence management in Salesforce Public Sector Solutions with Amazon S3&lt;/a&gt;, we used the External Storage of Files with Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) integration from &lt;a href="https://www.salesforce.com/government/solutions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Agentforce Public Sector&lt;/a&gt; (formerly Public Sector Solutions) as an example implementation. With that foundation in place, you now have durable, cost-efficient storage for body camera footage, surveillance video, photographs, audio recordings, and scanned documents.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;However, storage is only half the challenge. Without automation, you spend significant time manually reviewing, classifying, and extracting relevant details from these files before you can act on them. With this integration, Agentforce users can search for processed data stored on AWS, surface key insights from unstructured data, and perform more advanced actions, all without leaving the Salesforce console.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Two main flows work together to turn raw evidence into actionable investigative insights. The first flow moves unstructured media files and documents into Amazon S3 using the &lt;a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=ind.psc_external_file_storage_amazons3.htm&amp;amp;language=en_US&amp;amp;type=5" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;External Storage of Files with Amazon S3 for Public Sector&lt;/a&gt; connector. Figure 1 illustrates how Amazon S3 provides enterprise-scale storage infrastructure for storing large documents and media files.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div id="attachment_139426" style="width: 881px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-139426" loading="lazy" class="wp-image-139426 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/15/Solution_Overview.png" alt="" width="871" height="521"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-139426" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Agentforce Public Sector and Amazon S3 integration&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Second, after data is in Amazon S3, an event-driven architecture asynchronously processes multimodal data using Amazon Bedrock Data Automation. Figure 2 shows how you can extend the storage solution to create an architecture pattern. This pattern transforms unstructured data into actionable insights and makes them available to Salesforce Agentforce through MCP.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div id="attachment_139427" style="width: 871px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-139427" loading="lazy" class="wp-image-139427 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/15/Solution_OverviewMCP.png" alt="" width="861" height="801"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-139427" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Generating insights from unstructured data&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;As Figure 2 illustrates, when a file or document lands in Amazon S3, an S3 event notification invokes an AWS Lambda function. The Lambda function generates a document ID, stores it alongside document metadata in Amazon DynamoDB, and starts an Amazon Bedrock Data Automation job to process the file. Amazon Bedrock Data Automation extracts structured insights based on the media type. When the job completes, an Amazon EventBridge rule triggers a second Lambda function that saves the results to a dedicated output bucket in Amazon S3.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;On the Salesforce side, a user’s chat in Agentforce triggers a configured action that calls AWS over MCP. The call routes through Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, which authenticates the request and invokes an MCP server running on AWS Lambda. Amazon Bedrock AgentCore is the platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The Lambda function first queries the DynamoDB table to locate the relevant results. It then retrieves and returns them from Amazon S3. The results return through AgentCore Gateway to Agentforce, where the data is loaded into the agent’s context for a natural language response.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With Amazon Bedrock Data Automation, you can process each file based on its media type. For documents, it extracts text, identifies key fields, and generates structured summaries. For images, it produces descriptions and identifies objects or text within the frame. For video and audio files, it generates transcriptions and scene-level summaries. The Amazon Bedrock Data Automation project configuration defines which extraction capabilities to apply to each file type, and you can customize these settings in the Amazon Bedrock Data Automation console after deployment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This processing happens behind the scenes. Salesforce users can upload files, ask questions, and receive AI-powered insights entirely from the Salesforce console, without switching between systems or managing AWS resources directly.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This architecture is intentionally modular and extensible, designed as a pattern you can adapt well beyond evidence management. Each component, from the processing pipeline to the query path, operates independently and can be customized to your agency’s unique requirements. For example, you can add custom processing logic in the AWS Lambda MCP Serverless Runtime or store additional metadata in Amazon DynamoDB for richer document lookups. You can also connect different agent frontends through MCP without changing the underlying data pipeline.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="technical-implementation-guide"&gt;Technical implementation guide&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This section walks through deploying the AWS infrastructure and configuring Salesforce Agentforce to connect to the MCP endpoint.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before beginning, complete the steps outlined in the previous post, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/publicsector/modernizing-evidence-management-in-salesforce-public-sector-solutions-with-amazon-s3/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Modernizing evidence management in Salesforce Public Sector Solutions with Amazon S3&lt;/a&gt;, as this post builds directly on that foundation. Additionally, confirm that your Salesforce org supports registering and calling external MCP servers through the Agentforce Registry. You can verify this by navigating to &lt;strong&gt;Setup &amp;gt; API Catalog &amp;gt; MCP Server&lt;/strong&gt; and confirming the option to register an MCP server is available. Registering external MCP servers is available in Developer, Enterprise, Performance, and Unlimited Editions (see &lt;a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.api_catalog_manage_manual_external_mcp_servers.htm&amp;amp;type=5" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Manage External MCP Servers&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="deploy-aws-cloud-development-kit-aws-cdk-stack"&gt;Deploy AWS Cloud Development Kit (AWS CDK) stack&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-extending-public-sector-intelligence-with-Agentforce-and-AWS" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt; provides a deployment of the AWS resources required to create an event-driven architecture. The solution deploys a serverless infrastructure that includes Amazon EventBridge rules, Amazon Bedrock Data Automation configuration, AWS Lambda functions, Amazon DynamoDB tables, and Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway. This sample code is provided to demonstrate the pattern and isn’t production ready, so review and harden it to meet your organization’s requirements before using it in production.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After deploying the AWS CDK stack, configure Salesforce Agentforce to connect to the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway MCP endpoint. Agentforce connects to AgentCore Gateway using the MCP Streamable HTTP transport. With this connection, Agentforce can discover and invoke the evidence retrieval tools exposed by the gateway. The AWS CDK stack outputs several values you need to configure the connection between Salesforce and AWS. Retrieve these from the AWS Management Console before proceeding.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Optionally, before configuring the Salesforce connection, you can validate your gateway endpoint using the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/gateway-using-inspector.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;MCP Inspector&lt;/a&gt;, a developer tool for testing and debugging MCP servers through an interactive interface. This step isn’t required but can help confirm that your AgentCore Gateway is responding correctly before integrating it with Agentforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h4 id="step-1-get-aws-cloudformation-outputs"&gt;Step 1: Get AWS CloudFormation outputs&lt;/h4&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the Intelligent Media Processing solution is fully deployed, the outputs required to set up the MCP connections are available in AWS CloudFormation under the &lt;code&gt;McpGatewayStack&lt;/code&gt; outputs. As shown in Figure 3, the primary outputs are &lt;code&gt;CognitoClientId&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;CognitoTokenEndpoint&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;GatewayMcpEndpoint&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-3.png" alt="AWS CloudFormation stack outputs showing CognitoClientId, CognitoTokenEndpoint, and GatewayMcpEndpoint values" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: AWS CloudFormation outputs&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Agentforce authenticates with AWS through Amazon Cognito. You need the client secret from your Cognito app client to complete the MCP server registration in Salesforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h4 id="step-2-get-client-secret"&gt;Step 2: Get client secret&lt;/h4&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Open Amazon Cognito on the AWS Management Console.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;In &lt;strong&gt;User Pools&lt;/strong&gt;, choose the User pool name created by the AWS CloudFormation template.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Choose the app client that corresponds to this user pool.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Figure 4 displays the Amazon Cognito app client page, where you can find the Client secret.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-4.png" alt="Amazon Cognito app client page with the Client secret field highlighted" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: Amazon Cognito client secret&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="connect-agentforce-to-the-mcp-endpoint"&gt;Connect Agentforce to the MCP endpoint&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With the AWS credentials in hand, you can now register the MCP server in Salesforce. This establishes the authenticated link so Agentforce can call AWS tools.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h4 id="step-3-create-mcp-connection"&gt;Step 3: Create MCP connection&lt;/h4&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;In the Salesforce Setup console, open Quick Find and search for &lt;strong&gt;API Catalog&lt;/strong&gt;, then choose &lt;strong&gt;MCP Server&lt;/strong&gt; (see &lt;a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=platform.api_catalog_manage_manual_external_mcp_servers.htm&amp;amp;type=5" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Manage External MCP Servers&lt;/a&gt;).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;New&lt;/strong&gt;. Then choose &lt;strong&gt;Register MCP Server&lt;/strong&gt; to create a connection.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Name the MCP server &lt;code&gt;AwsBdaResultsMcp&lt;/code&gt; and set the description to &lt;code&gt;MCP server for accessing results from Amazon Bedrock Data Automation&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Take the values gathered from AWS in Step 1 and 2 and input them into their corresponding fields, as illustrated by Figure 5, then choose &lt;strong&gt;Create and Continue&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-5.png" alt="Salesforce Create Connection page for registering the MCP server with values from AWS" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: MCP Server create connection&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol start="5" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Follow the prompts. When you reach the MCP Server Allowlist, choose one or more of the available tools that you want to use and that were deployed with the AWS CDK. In production, scope the allowlist to only the tools your agent requires. See the Security considerations section.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Save&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;You have successfully connected your Amazon Bedrock AgentCore MCP server to Salesforce Agentforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="configure-agentforce-to-use-mcp"&gt;Configure Agentforce to use MCP&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Now that the MCP server is registered, you can add MCP tools to an existing Agentforce subagent, or create a new subagent. The following steps walk through creating a dedicated Agentforce subagent whose primary task is handling requests related to evidence retrieval. This subagent uses the MCP tools to query processed evidence stored in AWS and return insights to the user in natural language.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h4 id="step-4-add-mcp-tool-actions-to-your-agentforce-agent"&gt;Step 4: Add MCP tool actions to your Agentforce agent&lt;/h4&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To integrate an external MCP server, use the new Agentforce Builder. The following steps use the Employee Agent template. You can apply this same MCP integration to other agent types (such as Service Agent or Customer Agent), though the exact navigation and configuration options might vary. If you have an agent that was built using the legacy Agentforce Builder, follow this guide to &lt;a href="https://help.salesforce.com/s/articleView?id=ai.agent_setup_create_upgrade.htm&amp;amp;type=5" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Upgrade to New Builder&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;From the App Launcher, open Agentforce Studio, then select &lt;strong&gt;New Agent&lt;/strong&gt;. Select &lt;strong&gt;Agentforce Employee Agent&lt;/strong&gt; from the available templates, then name it &lt;code&gt;Case Agent&lt;/code&gt; or a name relevant to your use case.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;In &lt;strong&gt;Agentforce Builder&lt;/strong&gt;, create a new subagent. Enter &lt;code&gt;Media Processor&lt;/code&gt; as the name and the following as the description:
  &lt;br&gt;
  &lt;code&gt;Subagent that handles all questions related to files, documents, photos, images, videos, or audio attached to the current case. Retrieves AI-generated insights from processed media and responds in natural language.&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;ol start="3" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;Save&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;In the &lt;strong&gt;Media Processor&lt;/strong&gt; Subagent, under the &lt;strong&gt;Actions Available for Reasoning&lt;/strong&gt; section, choose &lt;strong&gt;Add action&lt;/strong&gt;, then select &lt;strong&gt;Add from Asset Library&lt;/strong&gt;. Search for the MCP tool you registered (searching &lt;code&gt;AwsBdaResultsMcp&lt;/code&gt; narrows the results to the relevant tools). Figure 6 shows the connected MCP selected under the &lt;strong&gt;Actions Available for Reasoning&lt;/strong&gt; section.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-6.png" alt="Agentforce Builder showing the registered MCP tool selected under Actions Available for Reasoning" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: Make MCP available for agent action&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol start="5" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Under &lt;strong&gt;Reasoning Instructions&lt;/strong&gt;, provide the subagent with instructions on what to do and how to reply. Use the following reasoning instruction template:
  &lt;br&gt;
  &lt;code&gt;Handle all questions about files, documents, photos, images, videos, or audio attached to the current case. Run &amp;lt;MCP_PLACEHOLDER&amp;gt; to retrieve processed insights. If no insights are available, inform the user the attachment has not yet been processed. Don't fabricate content about unprocessed files.&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;ol start="6" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;In place of &lt;code&gt;&amp;lt;MCP_PLACEHOLDER&amp;gt;&lt;/code&gt;, enter &lt;code&gt;@&lt;/code&gt; to reference a resource inline, then select the MCP associated with this subagent. Figure 7 shows the MCP referenced inline in the Reasoning Instructions.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-7.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-7.png" alt="Reasoning Instructions field with the MCP referenced inline for the subagent" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: Add MCP to subagent&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol start="7" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;With the configuration complete, choose &lt;strong&gt;Save&lt;/strong&gt; to preview the agent.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h4 id="step-5-test-and-validate-mcp-integration"&gt;Step 5: Test and validate MCP integration&lt;/h4&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With Agentforce Builder, you can preview the agent and how it responds to questions in the chat. To simulate the conditions of an employee asking questions in the Salesforce console, you can modify the &lt;strong&gt;Context Variables&lt;/strong&gt;. These variables represent the values that would be assigned to the agent’s context when a user works in the Salesforce console. Figure 8 shows the Preview panel’s Context Variables in the Agentforce Builder.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To test the agent, set the &lt;code&gt;currentRecordId&lt;/code&gt; context variable to the Record ID (the unique 18-character ID) of the case that you want to test. Then choose &lt;strong&gt;Apply and Restart Session&lt;/strong&gt;. This sample uses the &lt;strong&gt;Agentforce Employee Agent&lt;/strong&gt;. Other agent types might have different context variables preconfigured, so adjust accordingly.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 695px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-8.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-8.png" alt="Preview panel in Agentforce Builder with the currentRecordId context variable set for testing" width="685"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 8: Preview context variables&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To test the configuration of the agent and verify that it can make an MCP callout to AWS, perform the following:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;After you set the Context Variables to simulate a case that has media files uploaded to Amazon S3 and processed through this integration, open Preview. Enter a prompt that can trigger the subagent configured in Step 4, such as &lt;code&gt;Summarize the files for this case&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The test succeeds when the agent returns a summary of each item associated with the case record, as shown in Figure 9.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-9.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-9.png" alt="Agent preview returning a natural language summary of the media files associated with the case" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 9: Preview outputs&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol start="3" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;To see how the Agentforce agent produced this output, review the &lt;strong&gt;Summary&lt;/strong&gt; outputs. They provide a natural language summary of the trace, explaining the steps the agent took to handle the request (see Figure 10).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-10.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/09/11/ML-20933-10.png" alt="Summary trace showing the steps the agent took to retrieve insights through MCP" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 10: Summary of action&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;ol start="4" type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;If there are any changes needed to get the expected outputs, modify the prompt in Agentforce Builder and select &lt;strong&gt;Save&lt;/strong&gt; before testing again in Preview.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;After you are satisfied with the outputs, you can deploy this agent or subagent into the agent interfaces your organization uses.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="extend-this-pattern-to-your-own-use-case"&gt;Extend this pattern to your own use case&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The architecture demonstrated in this post is not limited to evidence management. You can apply the same modular pattern to build solutions for workflows that involve processing unstructured data, such as permits, benefits claims, or compliance reviews. The key components are the following:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon S3 for storage.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock Data Automation for processing.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway for MCP-based tool exposure.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Salesforce Agentforce for natural language interaction.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Each of these can be recombined and extended for use cases involving unstructured data. Because each component operates independently, you can replace the processing engine to match your agency’s requirements while keeping the same ingestion and MCP query layers. For document-heavy workflows such as permits, benefits claims, or tax forms, you can substitute the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/accelerate-intelligent-document-processing-with-generative-ai-on-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GenAI Intelligent Document Processing (IDP) Accelerator&lt;/a&gt; as an alternative processing engine. This keeps the same Amazon S3 ingestion and MCP query path. Additionally, because the MCP server is built on an open standard, you only need to build it once. MCP-compatible agents or systems can connect to the same endpoint, so you can reuse the same query layer across multiple applications beyond Agentforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Regardless of which processing approach you choose, the MCP query path remains the same. AgentCore Gateway exposes your processed data as tools that MCP-compatible agents can discover and invoke. This means that, in most cases, the architecture supports starting with a single use case and expanding to additional workflows without re-architecting the integration between AWS and Salesforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="security-considerations"&gt;Security considerations&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because this solution connects an AI agent to your data through an MCP server, review its security posture against your organization’s requirements before you move beyond a proof of concept. Under the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/compliance/shared-responsibility-model/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Shared Responsibility Model&lt;/a&gt;, AWS secures the underlying infrastructure, and you secure your implementation. As a starting point, consider which users can access the agent and which tools and actions it can invoke. Also remember that content the agent processes, such as text extracted from evidence, might contain hidden instructions that trick the agent into unintended actions. This risk is known as indirect prompt injection. To mitigate this risk, treat all content extracted from evidence as untrusted data, never as instructions for the agent. Apply input validation on retrieved content before it enters the agent’s context. Scope the agent’s available actions to the minimum required using the MCP allowlist. Use Amazon Bedrock Guardrails to filter or reject content that attempts to override agent behavior. For a broader framework on threats specific to large language models (LLMs), see the &lt;a href="https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This solution processes public sector evidence, so apply responsible AI controls before production. Amazon Bedrock Guardrails can filter harmful content and redact sensitive information such as personally identifiable information (PII). It can also run grounding checks that confirm responses stay grounded in the retrieved evidence rather than fabricated. These are examples, not a complete list. For authoritative guidance on securing agents and MCP tool access, follow &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/agentic-ai-security/introduction.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Security for agentic AI on AWS&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/best-practices.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore best practices&lt;/a&gt;, and apply &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/guardrails.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Guardrails&lt;/a&gt; with least-privilege controls.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Also, note that the accompanying sample code is intended to demonstrate this pattern and is not production ready. Review and harden it to meet your organization’s requirements before deploying to production.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To avoid ongoing charges, clean up your resources when you’re finished experimenting. For step-by-step commands to remove all deployed resources, see the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-extending-public-sector-intelligence-with-Agentforce-and-AWS#clean-up-the-cdk-stacks" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repo&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;You must also manually delete the Agentforce MCP connection in Salesforce.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because this is an event-driven, serverless architecture, you only pay for what you use. Processing costs are incurred only when evidence is actively uploaded and analyzed. Amazon S3 and Amazon DynamoDB storage costs are based on the amount of data stored, with no minimum commitments or upfront fees. For details, refer to the pricing pages for each service used.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This post demonstrated how to combine Amazon Bedrock Data Automation with the Model Context Protocol to process unstructured evidence and surface structured insights directly in Salesforce Agentforce. Using Amazon Bedrock Data Automation, the architecture automatically extracts text from documents, generates descriptions from images, and produces transcriptions from video and audio files.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This pattern extends well beyond evidence management to public sector workflows involving unstructured multimodal data. For guidance on adapting this architecture to your agency’s specific needs, refer to the &lt;em&gt;Extend this pattern to your own use case&lt;/em&gt; section earlier in this post.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The full sample code is available on the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-extending-public-sector-intelligence-with-Agentforce-and-AWS" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repo&lt;/a&gt;. You can also explore extending the solution with additional Amazon Bedrock Data Automation output types. Another option is to integrate Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases, the fully managed capability for Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG), to support RAG-based Q&amp;amp;A across large evidence collections.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
